# Management-Governance Module — NU (Synthesis)

**Ticker:** NU (Nu Holdings Ltd.) · **Date:** 2026-09-06 · **Line read:** NYSE:NU Class A ordinary shares, USD · **Reporting:** IFRS, fiscal year ends 31 December · **Jurisdiction:** Cayman Islands incorporation, SEC foreign private issuer (Form 20-F is the proxy-equivalent), operating banks in Brazil, Mexico and Colombia · **Data pool:** `data/NU/` (citation label; all reads resolved inside the frozen extraction generation `f9081efa…09f2be`).

**Mode:** FULL run. All thirteen specialists (`00`–`12`) completed; zero agents failed; no declared-gap agent. The nine-component composite formula applies with **no substituted components** — every one of `07`–`12` produced a score. `balance-sheet-survival` did not run in this chain, so `10` and `11` proceeded on their own read of the pool and said so.

## Abstract

Nu Holdings is run by the founder who built it, and he is both heavily invested and unaccountable: he owns 18.6% of the economics and 74.4% of the votes through twenty-vote shares. Capital has created per-share value rather than size — book value per share rose 72.8% in eighteen months and retained profit earned roughly 29% incremental returns. Pay is small at 3.18% of net income, yet no performance metric or weight is disclosed for any award. The sharpest flag is structural, not criminal: chair and chief executive are one person, the founder picks five of nine directors, and minority holders cannot call a meeting. Competent, economically aligned stewards inside a rights architecture giving outsiders almost nothing.

---

## 1. Stewardship Verdict

- **Verdict:** **Aligned & competent** — and read the qualifier, because it does not travel without it. *Alignment* here is **economic** (an 18.6% founder stake worth ~US$13.0bn, zero pledging, related-party dealing at 0.58% of revenue) and *competence* is **delivered** (net income to parent −US$364.6m in FY2022 → US$3,607.1m TTM to Jun-2026; the Mexican banking licence obtained). The flags are **not minor**: they are structural minority-rights flags, and they are what caps the rating at Watchlist. This verdict must never be restated as "good governance" or "shareholder-friendly" — the board and the audit file are clean; the *rights architecture* is not.
- **NON-NEGOTIABLE GATE: PASS.** All four conditions tested on evidence rows: (1) `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` triggered no hard disqualifier; (2) zero Critical red flags fired in this module; (3) no controller, CEO, CFO or board chair is graded Disqualifying (25 persons swept: 22 Clean, 3 Minor concerns, 0 Material, 0 Disqualifying); (4) no material undisclosed legal or regulatory matter — A9-10 Green, with the one undisclosed item (a BCB fine of R$12,500 ≈ US$2,300, 0.00002% of net worth) failing the materiality threshold by four orders of magnitude. **Gate PASS is reported with 160 of 179 checklist items answered.**
- **Hard disqualifier flagged (business-model/01)?** **N.** Verbatim from that scan: *"No disqualifier triggered."* · *"Any disqualifier triggered: **N**"* · *"**No lock is applied here.** This scan clears NU on all eight hard tests only."*
- **Management quality /100:** **65**
- **Capital allocation /100:** **73**
- **Incentive alignment /100:** **54**
- **Shareholder friendliness /100:** **50**
- **Disclosure candor /100:** **69**
- **People & network integrity /100:** **50** *(from `07`: raw 77, capped at 50 by RF-NET-003 band (b); 68 subjects on the Discovery Register, both discovery phases ran, zero subjects `disclosable-and-omitted`)*
- **Audit & assurance quality /100:** **65** *(from `08`)*
- **RPT & leakage risk /100 (INVERTED — higher = worse):** **22** *(from `09`)*
- **Contingent-liability risk /100 (INVERTED — higher = worse):** **32** *(from `10`)*
- **Accounting-forensics risk /100 (INVERTED — higher = worse):** **31** *(from `11`)*
- **Legal & regulatory risk /100 (INVERTED — higher = worse):** **15** *(from `12`)*
- **Governance risk /100 (INVERTED — higher = worse):** **55** — the RF-NET-003 band-(b) **floor of 55 binds**. The evidence-only read sits at about 50 (four Red board items, a 20:1 dual class, a 55.8-point control-vs-cash-flow wedge, against a clean RPT file, no pledging, no enforcement and no restatement), so the floor and the evidence are within five points of each other.
- **Data quality /100:** **82** *(from `00`: verdict **Sufficient**; 115 sources, 174 extracts, **zero extraction failures**; the FPI proxy-equivalent, five audited years, a quantified related-party note and Form 4 insider data all present)*
- **Overall usefulness /100:** **85**
- **Checklist coverage:** **160 / 179 answered (89.4%)** — **85 Green · 65 Amber · 10 Red · 19 Not Available / Not Applicable / Insufficient Data**
- **Insider ownership (one line):** insiders hold **21.99%** of shares outstanding (1,068,718,206 shares), of which the founder alone holds **18.6%** (906,212,615 shares, ~US$13.0bn) — **0% pledged**, and the observed 12-month insider record is **0 open-market buys / 4 sells, net −592,707 shares (~US$9.0m)**, every sale after a results release, never before [`FY25 Form 20-F, Item 7.A, pp.203–204`; `CIQ Public Ownership→Insider Trading export, events to 2026-08-26 — vendor export`].
- **Biggest governance signal (one line):** the founder's **74.4% of votes against 18.6% of the economics** — a 55.8-percentage-point wedge — combined with a veto over dividends, share issues, M&A and executive pay that survives until Class B voting power falls below 10% (it is 84.2% today), which is the single fact that makes every other governance strength unenforceable by an outside holder.
- **Checklist Risk = max(22, 32, 31, 15) = 32** — the binding risk is **Contingent-liability risk (`10`)**; the worst is never averaged away (§12).
- **Governance Score /100 = 60.** Inputs shown: `0.14×73 (CapAlloc) + 0.11×54 (Incentive) + 0.11×50 (ShFriendliness) + 0.10×69 (Candor) + 0.11×65 (MgmtQuality) + 0.09×65 (Audit) + 0.11×50 (People & Network) + 0.11×(100 − 55 GovRisk) + 0.12×(100 − 32 ChecklistRisk)` = `10.22 + 5.94 + 5.50 + 6.90 + 7.15 + 5.85 + 5.50 + 4.95 + 8.16` = **60.17 → 60**. No component was substituted; the six-component fallback does not apply.
- **Confidence-Adjusted Governance Score /100 = 46** (60 × 76 ÷ 100 = 45.6).
- **Governance Rating: Watchlist** (55–69 band). This is also where the RF-NET-003 conviction cap independently lands it — *no rating above "Watchlist" until the linkage is explained by primary evidence* — so the arithmetic and the cap agree rather than fight.
- **Confidence Score /100: 76.** Capped at 80 by `07`'s declared breadth overflow (~65 named subjects unswept under the 25/15 budget). The triage-anticipated cap of 70 for "legal-database sweep did not run" **does not bind** — both `07` and `12` executed and logged their sweeps.
- **Red-Flag Count: 6 · Critical Red-Flag Count: 0.**

## 1A. Module Disconfirmation

- **Strongest bear point:** the rights architecture is not a discount, it is a lock. A 20:1 dual class whose sunset needs Class B voting power below 10% against 84.2% today, a founder who designates and removes five of nine directors, a standing veto over dividends, share issues, mergers and officer pay, no shareholder right to nominate or to requisition a meeting, and a dormant staggered board that arms the moment founder control drops below 50% [`FY25 20-F, Item 10.B, pp.209, 214, 217–218`; `Item 6.A — Shareholder's Agreement, p.193`]. Shareholder friendliness scores 2 of 15 on voting rights for exactly this.
- **Strongest bull point (the steelman):** the conduct inside that architecture has been better than the architecture required. Independence is 77.8% when the controlled-company exemption required none; the audit committee is 100% independent and chaired by a designated financial expert who is a former PwC audit partner; the related-party balance is **US$265 thousand** at 30-Jun-2026 (0.0015% of total liabilities); there is **no royalty, no promoter loan, no ICD, no pledge and no related-party M&A across a ten-year sweep**; and the founder handed back a **US$422.6m** contingent share award and up to 2% of dilution for no consideration in 2022 [`05` §1, §4; `09` A5-02/03/04/09; `FY24 20-F, Item 6.E, p.288`].
- **Single killer risk:** the founder's veto meets a credit cycle. Non-performing loans have risen every year (6.7% → 7.8% → 7.9% → 8.6% of loans) while the Tier 1 ratio fell 16.2% → 14.4% → 13.4%, and the company began repurchasing stock inside that trend with US$28.8bn of undrawn card limits off balance sheet [`02` §3]. If capital has to be conserved against minority interests, no outside holder has a mechanism.
- **Disconfirming evidence already visible:** three items cut against the bear case and are recorded, not buried — the buyback was executed at an average of about **US$12.31**, below the contemporaneous price, and reduced the share count 0.51%; the CFO succession was pre-announced six weeks ahead with a named successor and a transition to 2026-08-31; and both 2026 AGM resolutions passed with **96.23%** of votes cast, with no proxy-advisor campaign found [`02`; `01` §2; `SEC Form 6-K, AGM results Ex-99.1, filed 2026-08-07`].
- **What would flip the verdict down:** a second unannounced senior departure, or the Q3'26 print showing the non-performing ratio and the Tier 1 ratio diverging further while repurchases continue.

## 2. Specialist Roll-Up

**Claim-fidelity pass (CLAUDE.md §3; `frameworks/MODULE_PIPELINE.md` four failure shapes).** Every verdict line below was checked against its source report for a dropped **qualifier**, a dropped **basis**, a dropped **build**, or a **hardened verdict**. Four corrections were made on the way up and are marked ⚠ in the table: (i) `07`'s two Minor grades are **exposure floors with no personal record**, so the basis travels in the same sentence; (ii) `02`'s "value-creative" carries its own qualification — *the record is short and the credit book has never been through a downturn*; (iii) `10`'s ~22.1%-of-net-worth figure is a **bound on a gross claim against 52 defendants jointly**, not Nu's exposure, and is never quoted without that basis; (iv) `01`/`earnings/04` attributed the Q1'26 guides to CFO Rob Livingston — the verbatim transcript names **Guilherme Marques do Lago**, and the attribution is corrected here (Section 3, item B).

## 2B. The Governance Checklist (assembled — Hard Rule)

All **179** registry items, in registry order, each appearing exactly once, mechanically matched by ID to its owner's Universal Findings Table row. Flags are **carried, not re-derived**; where two agents touched the same fact the owner's row wins and the disagreement goes to Section 3. Where an owner answered one item across several rows, the headline verdict is shown and the other prongs are named in the Finding cell.

---

## 3. Reconciliation

Five items were routed to this synthesis for adjudication rather than inheritance, plus two disagreements found in the fidelity pass.

**A. `07`'s RF-NET-003 cap on People & network integrity — is the linkage "live"? Adjudicated: YES, the cap stands at 50.** `07` capped itself at 50 and asked me to decide. The band-(b) trigger names *"an admitted insolvency or liquidation proceeding … live enforcement, or a credible fraud allegation."* Grupo Casas Bahia's *recuperação judicial* is Brazil's judicial reorganisation — the local equivalent of an admitted insolvency proceeding — filed by the debtor itself on/around **2026-08-16**, quantified at **R$17.3bn** across ten group companies, **three weeks before this report's as-of date and unresolved**. The reported departure of Calderón from that board *after* the filing does not extinguish a linkage that was live at the filing date and is live now, and treating a same-week exit as severance would reward exactly the timing the "smart rat" test exists to catch (`07` correctly found that test does **not** fire here, because he left *after* the event, not ahead of it, and there is no second company). The cap binds: **People & network integrity 50, Governance risk floor 55, no rating above "Watchlist."** ⚠ **Carry the basis at every layer (§3):** *no adverse record was found against this person on any swept axis* — he is a former PwC audit partner, a Brazilian registered accountant, and Nu's designated audit-committee financial expert; the grade reflects the linkage, never a record against him. I also affirm `07`'s adjudication that the flag fires **once**, on Calderón, and **not** on Douglas Leone: Sequoia's FTX exposure is a **pleading-stage private civil claim against an investor that lost US$210m to someone else's fraud**, with no regulator, no admission and no adverse ruling — a Minor exposure floor, not a band-(b) fact.

**B. The CFO attribution conflict — `06` is right; `01` and `earnings/04` are corrected.** `01` and `earnings/04_guidance-consensus` both attribute the Q1'26 effective-tax-rate and risk-adjusted-NIM guides to **CFO Rob Livingston**. The **verbatim Q1 2026 transcript (2026-05-14) names Guilherme Marques do Lago as CFO** and records zero occurrences of "Livingston" — Livingston's appointment was effective **2026-07-13**, two months after that call. **The guides were given by the outgoing CFO, Lago.** The substance of both promise tests is unchanged (the numbers, the dates and the outcomes are the same), so `01`'s 65 and `06`'s 69 stand. The candor point sharpens rather than softens: **the outgoing CFO set the guide, and the incoming CFO ran the call on which it was missed and left unmentioned** — a handover boundary is exactly where a quietly-dropped guide is easiest to lose. Every downstream reference must name Lago.

**C. `04`'s RF-OWN-004 instruction — accepted in full.** §24 Filter 6 was tested and is **negative**: state-owned shares 0.03%, one reported segment = 100% of revenue, and Nu is the top holding company, not a listed subsidiary of a value-maximising parent. **RF-OWN-004 is NOT emitted; the Filter-6 Shareholder-friendliness cap of 55 and Governance-risk floor of 55 are NOT applied, and no value-trap note goes to the valuation module.** Shareholder friendliness scores 50 on its own evidence, below the cap that does not apply — so the number is earned, not imposed. Findings **04-015 (20:1 dual class) and 04-016 (55.8pp wedge) are one fact and are priced once**, in the Governance-risk read; they appear as two checklist rows (A3-04, A3-03) because the registry tests them separately, not because the penalty is taken twice. A founder-controller who built the business and holds 18.6% of the economics is not the structurally unaligned owner §24 Filter 6 describes — but he *is* an entrenchment problem, and that is priced under Shareholder friendliness and Governance risk instead.

**D. The unannounced-departure cluster — `01` and `05` are the same finding, and together they are worse than either alone.** `01` found the **President & COO (Lahrech)** and the **CTO (Olivier)** vanished between the FY24 and FY25 20-F with no announcement. `05` independently found two more: **David Marcus lost his independence designation** between the same two filings while staying on the Compensation and People Committee (dropping it to 3-of-4 independent), and **Daniel Krepel Goldberg** — an independent director from April 2021 who became a permanent observer of the Audit and Risk Committee in December 2023 — **vanished between the FY23 and FY24 20-F**. That is **four unannounced senior status changes across three filing years: two executives gone, one director gone, one director's independence silently dropped.** No single one is Red. The pattern is a **disclosure-completeness habit**, not four coincidences, and it is why Disclosure candor is held at 69 rather than higher and A1-09 is Amber rather than Green. Reconciled view: the more conservative reading is adopted — this is a repeated practice, and the follow-up is whether the FY2026 20-F names a President/COO or confirms the role is eliminated.

**E. The two RF-DISC-001 / RF-DISC-002 uses — reconciled; three distinct fires, no double count.** They are different facts, correctly tagged, and I record them separately: (i) **`06` fired RF-DISC-002** on recurring "one-off" add-backs — share-based compensation, a cost present in every period on record (US$212.6m / US$272.4m / US$271.8m / US$216.8m H1'26), added back to Adjusted Net Income every year, plus a CSLL tax remeasurement management called *"a nonrecurring one-off event"* (+US$58.5m FY2025) that recurred the next period (+US$28.4m H1'26); **`06` explicitly tested RF-DISC-001 and did NOT fire it** — the defect is omission of the tax line, not an assertion the numbers contradict. (ii) **`10` fired RF-DISC-001** on a different fact entirely: a **14.0× gap** between the Note 25(d) figure of US$92,738k and the related narrative, i.e. commentary that does not reconcile to the note it points at — compounded by the FY2025 20-F signposting readers to "note 23" when the contingency note is note 25. (iii) **`10` also fired RF-CL-001** on the GMF placement defect. Reconciled: **RF-DISC-002 (from `06`) and RF-DISC-001 (from `10`) both stand as separate rows in the Red-Flag Register**; neither is inherited into the other agent's score, and `06`'s not-fired RF-DISC-001 is recorded as tested-and-not-fired so a downstream reader does not read `10`'s fire as `06`'s.

**F. Triage's anticipated sweep cap vs what the sweeps actually did — the specialists win.** `00` recorded that every network host was denied in this runtime and pre-declared **People & network integrity max 65, Legal & regulatory risk floor 40, Confidence max 70**. `07` ran and logged 68-subject discovery plus per-person sweeps with dated web citations; `12` ran and logged twelve company-level sweeps; `05` obtained the **AGM 6-K (filed 2026-08-07)** that triage named its single highest-value missing document. Reconciled on the evidence rows, not on the anticipation: **that cap does not bind.** A narrower, real coverage limit is priced instead — no primary Cayman registry pull (the register is non-public), no paid Brazilian court-docket terminal, no certified consolidated-sanctions query, no partner-level PCAOB sweep, and ~65 named subjects in `07`'s declared breadth overflow. Those are what hold Confidence at 76 rather than higher.

**G. The one number where two agents could have disagreed and do not.** `10`'s ~22.1%-of-net-worth GMF figure and `12`'s A9-10 Green look opposed. They are not: `10` says the claim is **disclosed but in the wrong place** (Item 8.A, not Note 25(c)); `12` says it **is disclosed somewhere in the filing**, which is what A9-10 tests. Both are right, and the conservative composite is the one adopted: **no undisclosed material litigation (gate condition 4 not tripped), but a real placement-and-completeness defect (RF-CL-001, High).**

No other material disagreements between specialists.

## 5. Stewardship Summary

**(a) Have they delivered on promises and created per-share value?** Mostly, on a sample the company itself keeps thin. Six promises were specific and dated enough to test: two kept (the risk-adjusted interest margin after credit losses recovered from 9.5% to 12.4%, past the level promised; the Mexican banking licence obtained roughly three years after application), one missed (the 15–20% effective tax rate, delivered at 14.2% and then dropped from the script), one missed for six quarters then met, one open and currently running better than guided, one not assessable. The delivered record behind that is not thin at all: net income to parent went from −US$364.6m (FY2022) to US$3,607.1m (TTM to Jun-2026), diluted earnings per share from −US$0.08 to US$0.734, and book value per share up 72.8% in eighteen months to US$2.743, with retained profit earning about 29.2% incremental returns. That is per-share value, not size — but it has never been tested through a credit downturn, and non-performing loans have risen every year (6.7% → 8.6%) while the Tier 1 capital ratio fell 16.2% → 13.4%.

**(b) Do incentives and ownership point at per-share value or at size?** Ownership does, emphatically; pay does neither. The founder's 18.6% economic stake (~US$13.0bn) is roughly **142 times the entire annual pay pool** and is unpledged, so his dominant incentive is the share price, not the salary — and in 2022 he surrendered a US$422.6m contingent award and up to 2% of dilution for no consideration. The formal incentive plan, though, attaches **no metric and no weight to any award**: bonuses rest on undisclosed "pre-established business goals" and long-term awards are time-vested RSUs. Nothing in it is size-based either — there is no revenue, EBITDA, headcount or deal metric anywhere — so the plan is best described as *absent*, not perverse. Aggregate pay of US$91.3m is 3.18% of net income and fell 4.9% while profit rose 45.6%.

**(c) Are minority shareholders protected, and is management candid when results are bad?** Protected, no. Candid, mostly yes. The rights architecture is the weakest part of this company's governance by a distance: 20 votes per Class B share with an unreachable sunset, five of nine directors designated by the founder, a standing veto over dividends, share issues, mergers and officer pay, no right to nominate or requisition, an exclusive Cayman forum, and a staggered board that arms the moment founder control drops below 50%. Voting rights score 2 of 15. On candor the evidence runs the other way: management volunteered an 8.2% margin trough with a basis-point decomposition before being asked, talked down its own record-low 17.6% cost ratio as "not a run rate" and was proved right one quarter later, and itemised three one-offs — two of them negatives — in answer to a hard question. What holds candor at 69 is that **the selectivity runs one way**: the guide being beaten was reiterated; the guide missed to the flattering side was never mentioned again, in the quarter the tax line produced 96.7% of the earnings beat. And four senior status changes across three filing years went unannounced.

**(d) The single most important reason to trust — or not trust — this team with capital.** Trust: the *conduct* inside a structure that required none of it. Independence is 77.8% when the controlled-company exemption required zero, the audit committee is 100% independent under a real financial expert, the related-party balance is **US$265 thousand**, there is no royalty, no promoter loan, no pledge, no related-party acquisition in ten years, no restatement in five, and five consecutive unqualified audit opinions. Not-trust: none of that is **enforceable** by an outside holder, and the same person decides whether it continues. Everything good here is a choice the founder is currently making, and a choice is not a protection.

## 5B. Peer Governance Benchmark

`business-model/08_competitive-map` supplies a peer set — **Itaú Unibanco S.A., Banco Bradesco S.A., Banco Santander (Brasil) S.A., Caixa Econômica Federal and Banco do Brasil S.A.**, named by Nu itself in its own risk factors — but the data pool contains **no peer governance disclosures**: no peer board-composition tables, no peer ownership or pledge data, no peer auditor-fee notes, no peer related-party notes, no peer AGM tallies. Every specialist that looked recorded the same affirmative absence (`03` searched and found only crowd-sourced pay estimates below tier 10 and did not use them; `05` recorded "No peer governance data in pool"; `08` could obtain no size-band audit-fee median).

## 5D. Analyst Follow-Up Questions

| Finding | Flag | Question(s) an analyst must answer before relying on the verdict |
|---|---|---|
| Chair = CEO = 74.4% controller (A1-02/03) | Red | Are the Lead Independent Director's powers written down anywhere a shareholder can read? Is separation on any board agenda? Does this affect minority holders differently in a credit downturn? |
| Three IDs with independence concerns (A1-04) | Red | Why did Marcus's designation lapse, why does he remain on the committee that sets pay, and did the board reassess every other director when it happened? Is the ex-CFO's advisory role to the Audit and Risk Committee time-limited? |
| 11 outside boards on one director (A1-06) | Red | Is board capacity tested at all — and by whom, given there is no nominating committee? |
| 20:1 dual class + 55.8pp wedge (A3-03/04) | Red | Is there any realistic path to the sunset (Class B below 10% of votes, against 84.2%) within a decade? Would the founder accept a time-based sunset? |
| KPMG regulator discipline (A4-13) | Red | Does the FY2026 20-F name the signing partner? Has KPMG Brazil closed the PCAOB Part II quality-control findings? Was Nu's own engagement inspected? |
| ETR guide missed then dropped (A7-04) | Red | Does the Q3'26 call restate an ETR expectation and explain the H1 undershoot? What is the reversal profile of the US$3,649.1m deferred tax asset, now 27.5% of parent equity? Is this recurring or one-off? |
| Colombia GMF claim (A7a-05) | Red | What is Nu Colombia's proportionate share? Will the FY2026 20-F move the item into the contingency note with its procedural posture? Is the admission of 2025-08-25 confirmed on the primary docket? |
| Casas Bahia exposure (A17-07) | Red | Has Calderón's board departure completed? Does the FY2026 20-F still list the seat? Does Nu's board intend to comment? Is his committee chairmanship being reviewed? |
| Zero pay metrics and weights (A6-03) | Amber | Will individual pay and metrics ever be disclosed voluntarily, given the FPI exemption? What did the bonus actually pay against in FY2025? |
| Four unannounced senior status changes (A1-09, A13-04/05) | Amber | Will the FY2026 20-F name a President/COO or confirm the role is eliminated? Is there a written disclosure policy on officer departures? |
| Civil provision +14.7× since Dec-2022 vs +75.6% customers; 32,703 PROCON/small-claims proceedings (A7a-11/13) | Amber | Is litigation intensity per million customers (≈117 → ≈250) a product-conduct problem or a scale artefact? Is it material to earnings? |
| Effective tax rate 11.78% vs 42.5% statutory (A8-10) | Amber | Which specific incentives drive the gap, are they time-limited, and what happens to earnings when they lapse? |
| TTM cash conversion −38.3% (AF-01/AF-11, RF-EQ-001 inherited) | Amber | Is the H1'26 operating-cash-flow swing a funding-mix effect (loans outgrowing deposits) or an accrual-quality signal? Cross-check `earnings/06`. |
| Note 28(a) counterparty unnamed while a director is its CEO (A5-08/10) | Amber | Who is the counterparty, what is the approved cap, and was the arrangement benchmarked? |
| 7 group entities with no stated purpose; 4 layers; only 6 of 34 entities named in the audited filing (A11-01/05) | Amber | What do the six paired US LLCs do, and why are they absent from every note? |

## 6. What Would Change The Stewardship Verdict?

| Current Verdict | What Would Strengthen It | What Would Weaken It | Data Needed |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Aligned & competent** (Governance Score 60; Rating **Watchlist**; Gate **PASS**) | A time- or ownership-based sunset added to the Class B shares, or any reduction of the founder veto; separation of chair and CEO, or published Lead-Independent-Director powers; named metrics and weights attached to executive awards; the President/COO seat refilled with an announcement, or its elimination stated; the Colombia GMF claim moved into the contingency note with a quantified or explicitly unquantifiable share; an ETR expectation restated on the Q3'26 call with the H1 undershoot explained; a founder or director **buying** stock in the open market (there has not been one purchase in the observed window); Calderón's Casas Bahia linkage closed | A fifth unannounced senior status change; the founder using the veto against a distribution or a capital raise; a related-party transaction appearing with an unnamed counterparty at scale; the Q3'26 print showing non-performing loans and the Tier 1 ratio diverging further while repurchases continue; a Material or Disqualifying grade landing on a controller or KMP (which would trip the gate); pledging appearing against the Class B stake; a restatement, an auditor change, or the Colombia claim being quantified against Nu at scale | The FY2026 20-F (Items 6, 7, 16C and Note 25) — for the President/COO seat, Marcus's designation, the signing partner, the fee note and the GMF placement; the Q3'26 transcript (due ~2026-11-12) — for the ETR restatement and the credit trend; the **2026 AGM poll tallies split public vs founder** — the only direct measure of minority sentiment; primary Brazilian court dockets for the Casas Bahia and PROCON matters; the Note 28(a) counterparty name and approved cap |

## 7. Note To The Final Synthesizer

- **The stewardship verdict is "Aligned & competent," and the single strongest piece of evidence for it is conduct the structure did not require:** independence at 77.8% when the controlled-company exemption required zero, a 100%-independent audit committee, a related-party balance of **US$265 thousand** at 30-Jun-2026, and a founder who handed back a **US$422.6m** contingent award and up to 2% of dilution for no consideration. **Do not harden that verdict into "good governance" or "shareholder-friendly"** — the flags are not minor, they are structural minority-rights flags, and they are what caps the rating.
- **Non-Negotiable Gate: PASS**, computed from all four conditions with 160/179 items answered. No hard disqualifier, zero Critical red flags, zero Disqualifying grades on any controller/CEO/CFO/chair, and no material undisclosed legal matter (A9-10 Green; the one undisclosed item is a R$12,500 BCB fine, 0.00002% of net worth).
- **Capital allocation created per-share value, not size:** diluted EPS −US$0.08 (FY2022) → US$0.734 (LTM Jun-26), book value per share +72.8% in eighteen months to US$2.743, retained profit earning ~29.2% incremental returns, and a first buyback executed at ~US$12.31 average that cut the share count 0.51%. **Carry the qualifier:** the record is short and the credit book has never been through a downturn — non-performing loans have risen every year (6.7% → 8.6%) while Tier 1 fell 16.2% → 13.4%, and repurchases began inside that trend.
- **Incentives and ownership point in opposite directions.** Ownership aligns him hard: 18.6% of the economics, ~US$13.0bn, **142× the entire annual pay pool**, zero pledged. The formal plan aligns nobody: **zero named metrics and zero weights** on any award. Nothing is size-based either — the plan is absent, not perverse. Pay is 3.18% of net income and fell while profit rose 45.6%.
- **The people read:** 25 persons swept — **22 Clean, 3 Minor concerns, 0 Material, 0 Disqualifying**. The riskiest is **Rogério Calderón, chair of the Audit and Risk Committee — Minor concerns on an exposure floor, with no adverse record against him personally**; the grade reflects his seat at Grupo Casas Bahia S.A., in judicial reorganisation over R$17.3bn since ~2026-08-16. **No open §24 Filter 1 signal exists** — `business-model/01` routed nothing and RF-MGT-005 is not emitted by any agent.
- **The checklist read: 89.4% coverage (160/179), 10 Reds.** They cluster in exactly two places — **board control** (chair=CEO, no independent chair, three IDs with independence concerns, one director on 11 boards) and **ownership structure** (20:1 dual class, 55.8pp wedge) — plus four singletons (KPMG firm discipline, the dropped tax guide, the Colombia claim's placement, the Casas Bahia linkage). **The binding Checklist Risk is Contingent-liability risk at 32** (`10`), the worst of the four; RPT 22, forensics 31, legal 15.
- **Biggest governance risk:** the founder's 74.4%-vs-18.6% wedge plus a veto over dividends, share issues, M&A and officer pay that survives until Class B voting power falls below 10% (84.2% today). Severity: **High, structural, permanent on any realistic horizon.** Everything else in this module is conduct that could change tomorrow at his discretion.
- **Hard disqualifier from `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` — verbatim: *"No disqualifier triggered."* · *"Any disqualifier triggered: N."* · *"No lock is applied here. This scan clears NU on all eight hard tests only."***
- **§24 rejector filters: none tripped.** Filter 1 (integrity) — nothing routed, no cap. Filter 2 (turnaround) — not a turnaround, RF-MGT-004 not emitted, no cap. Filter 4 (serial acquirer) — US$131.9m over five years, RF-CAP-004 not emitted, no cap. **Filter 6 (unaligned owner) — tested and NEGATIVE by `04`: RF-OWN-004 is NOT emitted, so no Shareholder-friendliness cap of 55, no Governance-risk floor of 55 from that source, and NO VALUE-TRAP NOTE goes to valuation.** Shareholder friendliness is 50 on its own evidence, below a cap that does not apply.
- **Partial-data caps: exactly two, both from `07`, both pointing at one score.** RF-NET-003 band (b) cuts People & network integrity from a raw 77 to **50**, floors Governance risk at **55**, and holds the rating at **Watchlist**. The declared breadth overflow (~65 named subjects) caps Confidence at 80 (applied: **76**). Real coverage limits that are priced but not capped: no primary Cayman registry pull (regime fact, not opacity), no paid court-docket or certified sanctions query, no PCAOB partner-level sweep (no partner is named in any filing), 15 E-B entities scoped not swept. **Triage's anticipated "sweep did not run" cap does NOT bind — both sweeps ran and are logged.**
- **Two attribution corrections to carry forward:** (i) the Q1'26 effective-tax-rate and NIM guides were given by **outgoing CFO Guilherme Marques do Lago**, not Rob Livingston — `01` and `earnings/04` have this wrong and the verbatim transcript is decisive; (ii) `10`'s ~22.1%-of-net-worth GMF figure is a **bound on a gross claim against 52 defendants jointly, not Nu's exposure** — every *measured* contingent liability is 0.352% of net worth.
- **Biggest missing data point (one item):** the **2026 AGM poll tallies split between founder and public shareholders**. The 6-K discloses 96.23% for the nine-director slate, but not the public-only split — in a company where one person holds 74.4% of the votes, that split is the only direct measure of minority sentiment, and it would turn A10-03 from an Amber derivation into a real answer.
- **Explicit handoff: this module SUPERSEDES the `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance` quick-read.** Where they overlap, the master synthesizer must treat **this module's governance verdict and scores as the primary governance read** — Governance Score 60, Confidence-Adjusted 46, Rating **Watchlist**, Gate **PASS**, 6 red flags / 0 Critical.
- **Boundary respected:** no fair value, no probabilities, no risk/reward, no rating in the §18 decision set, no sizing. The one number this module hands the valuation module is a **negative**: Filter 6 does not trip, so no value-trap discount is warranted on ownership grounds.

## 8. Simple Summary

- **Promises: mostly kept, on a sample the company keeps deliberately small.** Two of six testable promises were kept, one was missed, one was missed for six quarters then met. Nu publishes no revenue, profit or cost guidance at all, so it cannot miss targets it never sets.
- **Capital has created value per share, not just size.** Earnings per share went from −US$0.08 to US$0.734; book value per share is up 72.8% in eighteen months; retained profit earns about 29 cents on each extra dollar of equity. The first buyback was well priced at about US$12.31.
- **The pay plan rewards nothing in particular.** Not one performance measure or weight is attached to any award — bonuses rest on undisclosed goals and long-term awards are just time-vested shares. It is not an empire-building plan either; it is an absent one. Total pay is small: 3.18% of profit, and it fell while profit rose 45.6%.
- **Skin in the game is exceptional; buying is not.** Insiders own 22% of the company, the founder alone 18.6% (~US$13.0bn) with **nothing pledged**. But in the observed window there were **zero open-market purchases and four sales** (net ~US$9.0m) — every sale after results, never before.
- **Minority shareholders are not protected.** The founder has 74.4% of the votes on 18.6% of the money, picks five of nine directors, and can veto dividends, share issues, takeovers and executive pay. Outside holders cannot call a meeting or nominate anyone.
- **Management is mostly candid when results are bad — with one repeatable exception.** It volunteered its worst margin before being asked and talked down its own best cost number. But the tax-rate promise it *missed* in a flattering direction was never mentioned again, in the very quarter the tax line produced almost the entire profit beat. Four senior people changed status across three years with no announcement.
- **No hard disqualifier was flagged.** `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` cleared all eight tests: *"No disqualifier triggered."* Six red flags fired in this module, **none Critical**, and the governance gate **passes**.
- **This module is useful for the master synthesizer:** 89% checklist coverage on primary filings with zero extraction failures, and it supersedes the earlier one-page governance read.

`people_register.csv`

*`grade_basis` is mandatory and carries `own_record` or `exposure_via:{entity}` — the qualifier has to survive in the artifact that outlives the run (§3). Two of the three non-Clean grades are linkage-derived and carry no adverse record against the named individual.*


`red_flags.csv`

```csv
red_flag_id,trigger,severity,evidence,source_and_date,score_impact,follow_up
"RF-NET-003 (band b)","Live hop-1 cross-link from a register person to an entity carrying a Material-equivalent fact (A17-07)","High","Grupo Casas Bahia S.A. filed for recuperacao judicial - the local equivalent of an admitted insolvency proceeding - on/around 2026-08-16 over R$17.3bn across ten group companies. Hop-1 via Rogério Calderón Peres, chair of Nu's Audit and Risk Committee, who sat on that board and its audit and finance committees from Sep-2019 and departed in the same days. BASIS: the linkage. No adverse record was found against this person on any swept axis.","Metrópoles / Bloomberg Línea / Gazeta do Povo / Times Brasil (CNBC) / BPMoney, Aug-2026, read 2026-09-06; FY25 Form 20-F Item 6.A; 07 section 2.5","People & network integrity 77 -> 50; Governance risk floor 55; no rating above Watchlist","Has the departure completed, and will Nu's board comment on its audit-committee chair's outside seat?"
"RF-AUD-007","Regulator discipline of the audit firm or signing partner (A4-13)","High","Three adverse matters against KPMG the firm inside 12 months: PCAOB censure 2025-03-11 (US$3.0m; Rule 3211, AS 1301, Rule 2200 and quality-control violations); expanded PCAOB inspection report published 2025-09-25 for failed remediation; CVM settlement 2025-05-20 (R$1,782,000).
```
